last inequality holds by the Taylor expansion and
(
B.21
).

Now we first assume that the following statement holds: If
\|({\bf A}^{\rm T}\widehat{{\bf z}})_{\mathcal{M}\cup S}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-\delta
and
\lambda\geq 4R_{1}\tau_{1}\sqrt{\hbox{log}(p)/(n)}/\delta
, then

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{1}\leq\{\sqrt{r}\|{\bf C}_{r}^{-1}{\bf C}_{m-r}\|_{2}+\sqrt{m-r}+(2/\delta+1/2)\sqrt{k}\}\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{2}.

We then will have

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}-\lambda(\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\tau_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}}\{\sqrt{r}\|{\bf C}_{r}^{-1}{\bf C}_{m-r}\|_{2}+\sqrt{m-r}+(2/\delta+1/2)\sqrt{k}\}^{2}-(\alpha_{1}-\mu)\right]\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{2}^{2}.

Now because
n\geq 4\hbox{log}(p)\tau_{1}^{2}\{\sqrt{r}\|{\bf C}_{r}^{-1}{\bf C}_{m-r}\|_{2}+\sqrt{m-r}+(2/\delta+1/2)\sqrt{k}\}^{4}/(\alpha_{1}-\mu)^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle 0=\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}-\lambda(\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}\leq-(\alpha_{1}-\mu)/2\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 0.

The first inequality holds by the fact that
(\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}=\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\leq\|\widehat{{\bf z}}\|_{\infty}\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}
. Hence we have

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}=\lambda(\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}.

Now because
\|(\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{\infty}<1
,
we conclude that
\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}=\bf 0
. Hence,
\hbox{supp}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}})\subset\mathcal{M}\cup S
. This would prove the claim in the statement.

Thus,
we only need to show that if
\|({\bf A}^{\rm T}\widehat{{\bf z}})_{\mathcal{M}\cup S}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-\delta
and
\lambda\geq 4R_{1}\tau_{1}\sqrt{\hbox{log}(p)/(n)}/\delta
, then

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{1}\leq\{(\sqrt{r}\|{\bf C}_{r}^{-1}{\bf C}_{m-r}\|_{2}+\sqrt{m-r})+(2/\delta+1/2)\sqrt{k}\}\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{2}.

First from (
B.22
), we have

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}-\mu)\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}\|_{2}^{2}-\tau_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}}\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}})}{\partial\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}}-\frac{\partial q_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}})}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}^{\rm T}}{\bf A}\right\}\widetilde{{\bf v}}-\left\{\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})}{\partial\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}}-\frac{\partial q_{\lambda}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}})}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}^{\rm T}}{\bf A}\right\}\widetilde{{\bf v}}

(B.23)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda(\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}-\lambda\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}+\lambda(\widetilde{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widehat{\bm{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda(\widetilde{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}+\lambda(\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widetilde{{\bf v}}.

The second inequality holds by (
B.3
). Now since

\hbox{supp}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})\subseteq\mathcal{M}\cup S
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda(\widetilde{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}

(B.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathcal{M}^{c}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{S}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{S}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{S}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{S}\|_{1}-(\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{S}+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{S}-\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{S}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{S}\|_{1}-(\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}-\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}.

In addition,

\displaystyle\lambda(\widehat{{\bf z}}^{\rm T}{\bf A})\widetilde{{\bf v}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda\{({\bf A}^{\rm T}\widehat{{\bf z}})_{S}\}^{\rm T}\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}+\lambda\{({\bf A}^{\rm T}\widehat{{\bf z}})_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\}^{\rm T}\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}

(B.25)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda\|({\bf A}^{\rm T}\widehat{{\bf z}})_{S}\|_{\infty}\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}+\lambda\|({\bf A}^{\rm T}\widehat{{\bf z}})_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{\infty}\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}+\lambda(1-\delta)\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}.

Combine (
B.23
), (
B.24
), and (
B.25
),
we have

\displaystyle-\tau_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}}\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}-\mu)\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}\|_{2}^{2}-\tau_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}}\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}-\lambda\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}+\lambda\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}+\lambda(1-\delta)\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda 2\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}-\lambda\delta\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}.

Now because
\delta\lambda/2\geq 2\tau_{1}R_{1}\sqrt{\hbox{log}(p)/n}\geq\tau_{1}\sqrt{\hbox{log}(p)/n}\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}\|_{1}
, the above display implies

\displaystyle-2^{-1}\delta\lambda\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}\leq\lambda 2\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}-\lambda\delta\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}

which leads to

\displaystyle\delta\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}\leq(2+\delta/2)\|\widetilde{{\bf v}}_{S}\|_{1}.

Then

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bf v}_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{1}+\|\widetilde{\bf v}_{S}\|_{1}+\|\widetilde{\bf v}_{(\mathcal{M}\cup S)^{c}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bf v}_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{1}+\|\widetilde{\bf v}_{S}\|_{1}+(4/\delta+1)\|\widetilde{\bf v}_{S}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bf v}_{\mathcal{M}}\|_{1}+(2/\delta+1/2)\sqrt{k}\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\{\sqrt{r}\|{\bf C}_{r}^{-1}{\bf C}_{m-r}\|_{2}+\sqrt{m-r}+(2/\delta+1/2)\sqrt{k}\}\|\widetilde{\bf v}\|_{2}.

The last equality holds by using the same argument as those lead to
(
A.30
).
This completes the proof. ∎

Corollary
B.7
.

Assume Conditions
(C1)
–
(C6)
hold,
\lambda\leq\alpha_{1}/(8R_{1})
,
R_{1}\leq[n\alpha_{1}^{2}/\{64\tau_{1}^{2}\hbox{log}(p)\}]^{1/4}
,
\delta\in[4R_{1}\tau_{1}\sqrt{\hbox{log}(p)/n}/\lambda,1]
,
n\geq 4\hbox{log}(p)\tau_{1}^{2}\{\sqrt{m}+(2/\delta+1/2)\sqrt{k}\}^{4}/(\alpha_{1}-\mu)^{2}
, and
\alpha_{1}>\mu
. Suppose

\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{a}
is a stationary point of program (
8
) and

\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}
is the interior local minimize of (
8
) and satisfies

\hbox{supp}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})\subseteq\mathcal{M}\cup S
. Then
\hbox{supp}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{a})\subseteq\mathcal{M}\cup S
.

Proof: The Corollary holds by using the same arguments as
those lead to Lemma
B.13
, while using the consistency
result in Theorem
2
. ∎

B.4
Supporting Results related to Test Statistics

B.4.1
Some Definitions

We define

\displaystyle{\bf\Sigma}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle E[\{Y_{i}{\bf W}_{i}-\exp(\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}/2)({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})\}^{\otimes 2}]

By using the relation (
2
)–(
4
) and the
additional relations

\displaystyle E\{\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t})\mid{\bf X}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}),

\displaystyle E\{\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t})({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t})\mid{\bf X}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}){\bf X}_{i},

\displaystyle E[\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t})\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t})^{\otimes 2}-\bm{\Omega}/2\}\mid{\bf X}_{i}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}){\bf X}_{i}^{\otimes 2},

we have

\displaystyle{\bf\Sigma}(\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E[\{\exp(\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i})-\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i})\}({\bf X}_{i}{\bf X}_{i}^{\rm T}+\bm{\Omega})

\displaystyle-\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i})\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t}{\bf X}_{i}^{\rm T}-\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}){\bf X}_{i}\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}+\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t})({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t})^{\otimes 2}]

and the sample version

\displaystyle\widehat{\bf\Sigma}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\exp(\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}/2)\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})^{\otimes 2}-\bm{\Omega}\}

\displaystyle-\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})^{\otimes 2}-\bm{\Omega}/2\}

\displaystyle+\exp(\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}/2)\bm{\Omega}-\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})\bm{\Omega}

\displaystyle-\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})^{\rm T}-\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}

\displaystyle+\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\exp(\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}/2)\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})^{\otimes 2}\}

\displaystyle-\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})^{\otimes 2}+\bm{\Omega}/2\}

\displaystyle-\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})^{\rm T}-\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}

\displaystyle+\exp(2\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta})^{\otimes 2}.

Further, let

\displaystyle{\bm{\Psi}}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta})=({\bf C}[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]{{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}({\bm{\beta}}){\bf\Sigma}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}({\bm{\beta}}){{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}({\bm{\beta}})[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]^{\rm T}{\bf C}^{\rm T}),

and

\displaystyle T_{0}=(\bm{\omega}_{n}+\sqrt{n}{\bf h}_{n})^{\rm T}\bm{\Psi}^{-1}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t})(\bm{\omega}_{n}+\sqrt{n}{\bf h}_{n}),

where

\displaystyle\bm{\omega}_{n}=-\sqrt{n}{\bf C}[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]{{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}(\bm{\beta}_{t})\left\{\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}(\bm{\beta}_{t})}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{t}}\right\}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S}

Further define Wald statistics as

\displaystyle T_{W}=n({\bf C}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a\mathcal{M}}-{\bf t})^{\rm T}{\bm{\Psi}}(\widehat{\bf\Sigma},\widehat{\bf Q},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})^{-1}({\bf C}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a\mathcal{M}}-{\bf t});

the score statistics

\displaystyle T_{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\left\{\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})}{\partial\bm{\beta}^{\rm T}}\right\}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S}({\bf C}[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]{{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}))^{\rm T}

\displaystyle\times\bm{\Psi}^{-1}(\widehat{\bf\Sigma},\widehat{\bf Q},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}){\bf C}[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]{{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})\left\{\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})}{\partial\bm{\beta}}\right\}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S}.

Because without knowing the distribution of
{\bf X}
, the full likelihood
is unknown and hence we do not discuss the likelihood ratio test
here.

B.4.2
Assumptions

(D1)

Assume

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}(\|\bm{\Psi}^{-1/2}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t}){\bf C}[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]{{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}(\bm{\beta}_{t}){\bf W}_{i\mathcal{M}\cup S}\|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle+\|\bm{\Psi}^{-1/2}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t}){\bf C}[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]{{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}(\bm{\beta}_{t})(\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t})_{\mathcal{M}\cup S}\|_{2}^{3})^{-1}

\displaystyle\times E[\|Y_{i}\bm{\Psi}^{-1/2}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t}){\bf C}[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]{{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}(\bm{\beta}_{t}){\bf W}_{i}-\exp(\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{t}/2)

\displaystyle\times\bm{\Psi}^{-1/2}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t}){\bf C}[{\bf I}_{m\times m},{\bf 0}_{m\times k}]{{\bf Q}}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}^{-1}(\bm{\beta}_{t})({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{t}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega})_{\mathcal{M}\cup S}\|_{2}^{3}|{\bf W}_{i}]=O(1)

(D2)

c_{\bm{\Psi}}\leq\alpha_{\min}\{{\bm{\Psi}}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta})\}\leq\alpha_{\max}\{{\bm{\Psi}}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta})\}\leq C_{\bm{\Psi}}.

B.4.3
Some Lemmas

Lemma
B.14
.

Suppose
{\bf X}_{1},\ldots,{\bf X}_{n}
are independent
m
dimensional random
vector which satisfies,
E(X_{i})=0
and
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hbox{cov}({\bf X}_{i})={\bf I}_{m}
. Let

{\bf Z}
be a m-dimensional standard multivariate normal vector, then

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathcal{C}}|\Pr(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bf X}_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}})-\Pr({\bf Z}\in\mathcal{C})|=O(m^{1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\|{\bf X}_{i}\|_{2}^{3}).

Proof: The lemma follows Theorem 1.1 in

Bentkus 2005
.

Lemma
B.15
.

Assume Conditions
(C1)
–
(C7)
hold, then

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\Psi}}^{-1}(\widehat{\bf\Sigma},\widehat{\bf Q},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})-{\bm{\Psi}}^{-1}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t})\|_{2}=\left\{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}\right\}^{1/4}(m+k),

and

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\Psi}}^{-1}(\widehat{\bf\Sigma},\widehat{\bf Q},\widehat{\bm{\beta}})-{\bm{\Psi}}^{-1}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t})\|_{2}=\left\{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}\right\}^{1/4}(m+k).

Proof: First

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\Psi}}(\widehat{\bf\Sigma},\widehat{\bf Q},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})-{\bm{\Psi}}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t})\|_{2}

(B.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{p}\{\max(\|\widehat{\bf Q}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})^{-1}-{\bf Q}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}({\bm{\beta}_{t}})^{-1}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bf\Sigma}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})-{\bf\Sigma}_{\mathcal{M}\cup S,\mathcal{M}\cup S}({\bm{\beta}_{t}})\|_{2}\}.

Using the same arguments as those lead to (
A.53
), we
have
\|\widehat{\bf Q}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})^{-1}-{\bf Q}({\bm{\beta}_{t}})^{-1}\|_{2}=\left\{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}\right\}^{1/4}(m+k)
.
Now recall that
{\mathbb{K}}\equiv\{{\bf v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathcal{M}\cup S}:\|{\bf v}\|_{2}\leq 1\}
, for
{\bf v}\in{\mathbb{K}}
,
by the Taylor expansion

\displaystyle{\bf v}^{\rm T}\{\widehat{\bf\Sigma}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})-\widehat{{\bf\Sigma}}({\bm{\beta}_{t}})\}{\bf v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\exp(\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}/2){\bf v}^{\rm T}\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\otimes 2}\}{\bf v}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}/2){\bf v}^{\rm T}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\otimes 2}+\bm{\Omega}/2\}{\bf v}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}{\bf v}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})\{{\bf v}^{\rm T}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}-{\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}{\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}{\bf v}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})\{{\bf v}^{\rm T}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}-{\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}{\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\otimes 2}\}{\bf v}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\exp(\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}/2){\bf v}^{\rm T}\{({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\otimes 2}\}{\bf v}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}/2){\bf v}^{\rm T}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}{\bf v}/2({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}4\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}{\bf v}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})^{\rm T}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp(2\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}{\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{a}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a})\{{\bf v}^{\rm T}({\bf W}_{i}-\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}){\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}-{\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\bm{\beta}_{a}{\bf v}^{\rm T}\bm{\Omega}\}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}-\bm{\beta}_{t}),

where
\bm{\beta}_{a}
is a point in between
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a}
and
\bm{\beta}_{t}
. Using
the same arguments as those lead to (
A.45
) and (
A.46
), we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bf\Sigma}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})-\widehat{{\bf\Sigma}}({\bm{\beta}_{t}})\|_{2}=O_{p}\left[\left\{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}\right\}^{1/4}(m+k)\right],

and

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bf\Sigma}(\bm{\beta})-{{\bf\Sigma}}({\bm{\beta}_{t}})\|_{2}=o_{p}\left[\left\{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}\right\}^{1/4}(m+k)\right].

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bf\Sigma}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})-\widehat{{\bf\Sigma}}({\bm{\beta}_{t}})\|_{2}=O_{p}\left[\left\{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}\right\}^{1/4}(m+k)\right].

Combine with (
B.26
) and the fact that
\|\widehat{\bf Q}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})^{-1}-{\bf Q}({\bm{\beta}_{t}})^{-1}\|_{2}=\left\{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}\right\}^{1/4}(m+k)
, by Lemma

B.11
we have

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\Psi}}^{-1}(\widehat{\bf\Sigma},\widehat{\bf Q},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})-{\bm{\Psi}}^{-1}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{p}\{\|{\bm{\Psi}}(\widehat{\bf\Sigma},\widehat{\bf Q},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{a})-{\bm{\Psi}}({\bf\Sigma},{\bf Q},\bm{\beta}_{t})\|_{2}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\hbox{log}(p)}{n}\right\}^{1/4}(m+k).

The second relation in the statement holds by using the same arguments as those lead
the above results. This proves the result.∎

B.5
The composite ROIs

Based on
Braak & Braak 1991
,
Landau et al. 2016
,
Schöll et al. 2016
, we define the
composite regions as follow, where letter
L
and
R
represent the
left and right hemispheres, respectively.

•

Braak 1 and 2 composite region (Braak12):

L_entorhinal, R_entorhinal

•

Braak 3 and 4 composite region (Braak34):

L_parahippocampal, L_fusiform,
L_lingual, L_amygdala, R_parahippocampal, R_fusiform, R_lingual,
R_amygdala, L_middletemporal, L_caudantcing, L_rostantcing,
L_postcing, L_isthmuscing, L_insula, L_inferiortemporal, L_temppole,
R_middletemporal, R_caudantcing, R_rostantcing, R_postcing,
R_isthmuscing, R_insula, R_inferiortemporal, R_temppole.

•

Braak 5 and 6 composite region (Braak56):

L_superior_frontal, L_lateral_orbitofrontal, L_medial_orbitofrontal,

L_frontal_pole, L_caudal_middle_frontal, L_rostral_middle_frontal,
L_pars_opercularis, L_pars_orbitalis, L_pars_triangularis,
L_lateraloccipital, L_parietalsupramarginal, L_parietalinferior,
L_superiortemporal, L_parietalsuperior, L_precuneus,
L_bankSuperiorTemporalSulcus, L_tranvtemp, R_superior_frontal,
R_lateral_orbitofrontal, R_medial_orbitofrontal, R_frontal_pole,
R_caudal_middle_frontal, R_rostral_middle_frontal, R_pars_opercularis,
R_pars_orbitalis, R_pars_triangularis, R_lateraloccipital,
R_parietalsupramarginal, R_parietalinferior,

R_superiortemporal,
R_parietalsuperior, R_precuneus, R_bankSuperiorTemporalSulcus,
R_tranvtemp, L_pericalcarine, L_postcentral, L_cuneus, L_precentral,
L_paracentral, R_pericalcarine, R_postcentral, R_cuneus,
R_precentral, R_paracentral

B.6
Simulations when measurement error covariance is
unknown

To evaluate the proposed adjustment estimator in Section
6
, we added the
following three
simulation settings where the covariance matrices of the measurement
errors contain different numbers of unknown parameters.

1.

\bm{\Omega}
is a matrix with
p/4
unknown
parameters, corresponding to
p/4
nonzero diagonal entries.

2.

\bm{\Omega}
is a matrix with
p/2
unknown parameters,
corresponding to
p/2
nonzero entries.

3.

\bm{\Omega}
is a matrix with
6p-15
unknown
parameters. Specifically,

\bm{\Omega}=(\sigma_{ij})_{p\times p}
, where

\sigma_{ij}=0.05(1-|i-j|/5)
for
|i-j|\leq 5
and

\sigma_{ij}=0
for
|i-j|>5
.

In these settings, the number of unknown
parameters in
\bm{\Omega}

increases, while all
other settings are the same as in Section

5.2
. We evaluate the empirical sizes and powers of
the Wald test for
n=300
,
p=50
in Table
B.9
and
p=350
in Table
B.10
.
In the first two settings, where
\bm{\Omega}
contains relatively few
parameters hence their convergence is
sufficiently fast, the
empirical sizes are well controlled around the
nominal level 0.05 in all hypothesis tests. In the last setting,
the parameter number is too large to achieve sufficiently
fast convergence, hence
the Wald test cannot control the Type I errors. These results
imply that the proposed adjustment can control Type I error rate
when the error covariance matrix contains small or
moderate number of unknown parameters.
